# TERRAN — sources

Every source cited in the research behind the Terran Charter: 715 records, grouped by the Part of the Charter they inform. Generated from `sources/*.yaml` by `tools/build_sources.py`; do not edit by hand.

**Tiers:** 487 primary · 201 secondary · 27 tertiary. **Checks:** 594 verified · 111 partial · 10 unverified.

Every check names who made it and when. *Verified* means the cited claim was found on the fetched page; *partial* means only part of it, or only an abstract or landing page, could be confirmed; *unverified* means the page could not be opened. Checks by `claude` are machine-performed and are not Kara's own verification.

## Preamble

- [**Humanist Manifesto I**](https://americanhumanist.org/humanism/humanist-manifesto-i/) — American Humanist Association · 1933  
  Critique of 'acquisitive and profit-motivated society'; call for 'a socialized and cooperative economic order'; 'man is a part of nature'; now superseded by Manifesto III.  
  <sub>`aha/1933_humanist-manifesto-i` · primary · web · verified 2026-10-10 by claude · cited in [lineage](research/lineage.md)</sub>
- [**Humanist Manifesto II**](https://americanhumanist.org/humanism/humanist-manifesto-ii/) — Paul Kurtz, Edwin H. Wilson · American Humanist Association · 1973  
  World community and transnational federal government, keeping national pride; no single state ideology; guaranteed annual income; reducing wealth disproportions; single ecosystem and population growth.  
  <sub>`aha/1973_humanist-manifesto-ii` · primary · web · verified 2026-10-10 by claude · cited in [lineage](research/lineage.md)</sub>
- [**Humanist Manifesto III: Humanism and Its Aspirations**](https://americanhumanist.org/humanism/humanist-manifesto-iii/) — American Humanist Association · 2003  
  Values subject to change as knowledge advances; 'global community'; just distribution of nature's resources; planetary duty to protect nature; 22 Nobel laureate signers.  
  <sub>`aha/2003_humanist-manifesto-iii` · primary · web · verified 2026-10-10 by claude · cited in [lineage](research/lineage.md)</sub>
- [**About us**](https://thecommonwealth.org/about-us) — Commonwealth Secretariat · 2026  
  The Commonwealth as a voluntary association of 56 countries, home to 2.7 billion people.  
  <sub>`commonwealth/2026_about-us` · primary · web · verified 2026-10-10 by claude · cited in [lineage](research/lineage.md)</sub>
- [**Commonwealth Charter**](https://thecommonwealth.org/charter) — Commonwealth Secretariat · 2026  
  Existence of a 'Commonwealth Charter' for an association of sovereign states.  
  <sub>`commonwealth/2026_charter` · primary · web · verified 2026-10-10 by claude · cited in [lineage](research/lineage.md)</sub>
- [**What is the Doughnut?**](https://doughnuteconomics.org/doughnut) — Doughnut Economics Action Lab · 2026  
  Current DEAL framing: 12 dimensions, 22 social and 13 ecological indicators; median 2022 shortfall 35% of people (about 3 billion); six of nine boundaries transgressed by 2022; shortfall reported per indicator.  
  <sub>`deal/2026_what-is-the-doughnut` · primary · web · verified 2026-10-10 by claude · cited in [floor-and-ceiling](research/floor-and-ceiling.md)</sub>
- [**The Earth Charter**](https://earthcharter.org/read-the-earth-charter/) — Earth Charter Commission (published by Earth Charter International) · 2000-06-29  
  Preamble language; four pillars and 16 principles; precautionary burden of proof (principle 6); a common standard for all institutions; nations kept; population (principle 7).  
  <sub>`earth-charter/2000_earth-charter` · primary · web · verified 2026-10-10 by claude · cited in [lineage](research/lineage.md)</sub>
- [**FAQs**](https://earthcharter.org/about-the-earth-charter/faqs/) — Earth Charter International · 2024  
  The Earth Charter as 'a people's treaty' and 'a soft law document'; ECI based at the University for Peace, Costa Rica.  
  <sub>`earth-charter/2024_faqs` · primary · web · verified 2026-10-10 by claude · cited in [lineage](research/lineage.md)</sub>
- [**History**](https://earthcharter.org/about-the-earth-charter/history/) — Earth Charter International · 2024  
  Drafting launched in 1994 by Strong and Gorbachev; text agreed at UNESCO in March 2000; launch on 29 June 2000; over 2,000 organizational endorsements, including UNESCO and IUCN.  
  <sub>`earth-charter/2024_history` · primary · web · verified 2026-10-10 by claude · cited in [lineage](research/lineage.md)</sub>
- [**The Amsterdam Declaration 2022**](https://humanists.international/what-is-humanism/the-amsterdam-declaration/) — Humanists International · 2022  
  Reason and compassion; duty of care to future generations and sentient beings; rule of law; 'no one is infallible'; refusal to impose its view; adoption at the Glasgow General Assembly.  
  <sub>`humanists-international/2022_amsterdam-declaration` · primary · web · verified 2026-10-10 by claude · cited in [lineage](research/lineage.md)</sub>
- [**Humanist Manifesto 2000: A Call for a New Planetary Humanism**](https://web.archive.org/web/20060904194544/http://www.secularhumanism.org/index.php?section=main&page=manifesto) — Paul Kurtz · Council for Secular Humanism · 2000  
  'Planetary Humanism'; a Planetary Bill of Rights going beyond the UDHR; UN World Parliament, income tax for poorer countries, end of the veto, environmental agency, world court; free markets as one strategy.  
  <sub>`kurtz/2000_humanist-manifesto-2000` · primary · web · verified 2026-10-10 by claude · cited in [lineage](research/lineage.md)</sub>
- [**What We Owe The Future (book site)**](https://whatweowethefuture.com/) — William MacAskill · Basic Books · 2022  
  Humanity's future could last millions more years or end tomorrow.  
  <sub>`macaskill/2022_what-we-owe-the-future` · primary · web · verified 2026-10-10 by claude · cited in [long-view](research/long-view.md)</sub>
- [**The Precipice: Existential Risk and the Future of Humanity (book site FAQ)**](https://theprecipice.com/faq) — Toby Ord · theprecipice.com · 2020  
  Ord's best guess of a one-in-six chance of existential catastrophe over the next century; natural risk about 1 in 10,000 per century.  
  <sub>`ord/2020_the-precipice` · primary · web · verified 2026-10-10 by claude · cited in [long-view](research/long-view.md)</sub>
- [**Planetary Health Check 2026 finds mounting pressures across Earth's life support systems**](https://www.pik-potsdam.de/en/news/latest-news/planetary-health-check-2026-finds-mounting-pressures-across-earth2019s-life-support-systems) — Potsdam Institute for Climate Impact Research · 2026-09-21  
  Seven of nine crossed, all at highest recorded transgression; more than 60 authors; risk of large-scale, potentially irreversible change; Planetary Guardians' mission to make boundaries 'a measurement and operating framework for the world'.  
  <sub>`pik/2026_phc-press-release` · primary · web · verified 2026-10-10 by claude · cited in [floor-and-ceiling](research/floor-and-ceiling.md)</sub>
- [**A Safe and Just Space for Humanity: Can We Live Within the Doughnut?**](https://policy-practice.oxfam.org/resources/a-safe-and-just-space-for-humanity-can-we-live-within-the-doughnut-210490/) — Kate Raworth · Oxfam International · 2012-02  
  First doughnut; 11 illustrative social dimensions from Rio+20 submissions; voice, jobs and resilience 'To be determined'; both rings 'essentially normative'; poverty arithmetic (1% of food supply, 0.2% of income).; Origin of the Preamble's phrase 'a safe and just space for humanity'.  
  <sub>`raworth/2012_safe-and-just-space` · primary · report · verified 2026-10-10 by claude · cited in [floor-and-ceiling](research/floor-and-ceiling.md), [lineage](research/lineage.md)</sub>

- [**Planetary Boundaries: Exploring the Safe Operating Space for Humanity**](https://ecologyandsociety.org/vol14/iss2/art32/) — Johan Rockström, Will Steffen, Kevin Noone · Ecology and Society · 2009  
  Boundaries are 'human-determined values' at a safe distance from danger; setting that distance 'involves normative judgments of how societies choose to deal with risk and uncertainty'.  
  <sub>`rockstrom/2009_planetary-boundaries-ecology-society` · primary · article · verified 2026-10-10 by claude · cited in [floor-and-ceiling](research/floor-and-ceiling.md)</sub>

  Original nine planetary boundaries, published 23 September 2009.  
  <sub>`rockstrom/2009_safe-operating-space` · primary · article · partial 2026-10-10 by claude: Paywalled; landing page only (publication date, author list, standfirst). Nature 461:472-475; 29 authors in total. · cited in [floor-and-ceiling](research/floor-and-ceiling.md)</sub>
- [**Safe and just Earth system boundaries**](https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC10322705/) — Johan Rockström, Joyeeta Gupta, Dahe Qin · Nature · 2023-05-31  
  Safe vs just boundaries; stricter governs; just climate boundary at or below 1.0 C vs safe 1.5 C; PM2.5 15 ug/m3; nitrogen surplus 57 vs 61 Tg N/yr; seven of eight exceeded; necessary but not sufficient; corridor; interspecies justice; proposals open to debate; Future Earth nominations; Paris 1.5 C guardrail.  
  <sub>`rockstrom/2023_safe-and-just-esbs` · primary · article · verified 2026-10-10 by claude · cited in [floor-and-ceiling](research/floor-and-ceiling.md)</sub>

  World community and transnational federal government, keeping national pride; no single state ideology; guaranteed annual income; reducing wealth disproportions; single ecosystem and population growth.

  <sub>`anderson/1983_imagined-communities` · primary · book · partial 2026-10-10 by claude: Only a Verso blog post (October 2016) about the book was reachable; it confirms the book's standing but not the print thesis. Book not opened. · cited in [commonwealth](research/commonwealth.md)</sub>

- [**A Constitution for the Federation of Earth**](https://www.wcpa.global/en/constitution-for-the-federation-of-earth) — World Constitution and Parliament Association (text hosted by the Earth Constitution Institute) · 1977  
  Article 10: enforcement applies to individuals, including officials and corporate agents; police armed only to apprehend; searches and arrests on warrants from World Attorneys; attorneys answer to Parliament; non-military means. Article 9: judges elected for renewable ten-year terms. Article 11: Ombudsmus.; Adoption at Innsbruck (June 1977) and Troia amendments (May 1991); three-house parliament; Presidium and Cabinet with no veto; World Ombudsmus; warrant-based police armed only for apprehension; stage thresholds and disarmament by stage; paired ratification; non-aggression pledge; 25% turnout rule; exit zone after 90% of territory; 1995 stall review.; Art. VI: Presidium of 5 and Executive Cabinet of 20-30, all from parliament; one Presidium member per continental division; elected by parliament from nominees; 5-year terms, at most two in a row; removable for cause; no veto, no dissolution of parliament, no defiance of courts. Art. XI: council of five World Ombudsmen, elected by parliament, 10-year terms, may start court cases.; Arrest-only police; staged joining (WMD first, militaries last); also shows the text's own exit right.; World government may 'establish, issue and regulate world currency, credit and exchange' (Art. IV.17); a Planetary Banking System makes 'the transition to a common global currency' and funds government with interest-free credit (Art. VIII, Sec. G-1(e)-(f)).  
  <sub>`wcpa/1977_earth-constitution` · primary · report · verified 2026-10-10 by claude · cited in [accountability](research/accountability.md), [commonwealth](research/commonwealth.md), [deliberation](research/deliberation.md), [drafting-session-checks](research/drafting-session-checks.md), [money](research/money.md)</sub>
- [**Why a World State Is Inevitable: Teleology and the Logic of Anarchy**](https://www.comw.org/qdr/fulltext/03wendt.pdf) — Alexander Wendt · European Journal of International Relations · 2003  
  A world state (global monopoly on legitimate organized violence) is inevitable, guessed within 100 years; it need not end local autonomy and could keep national armies; collective security cannot stop secession and rearmament.  
  <sub>`wendt/2003_world-state-inevitable` · primary · article · verified 2026-10-10 by claude · cited in [commonwealth](research/commonwealth.md)</sub>

  Founding principles: universal membership, limited national sovereignty, world law enforced on individuals, supranational armed forces, member disarmament to policing levels.

## Part II — The Seven Principles

- [**Humanist Manifesto I**](https://americanhumanist.org/humanism/humanist-manifesto-i/) — American Humanist Association · 1933  
  Critique of 'acquisitive and profit-motivated society'; call for 'a socialized and cooperative economic order'; 'man is a part of nature'; now superseded by Manifesto III.  
  <sub>`aha/1933_humanist-manifesto-i` · primary · web · verified 2026-10-10 by claude · cited in [lineage](research/lineage.md)</sub>
- [**Humanist Manifesto II**](https://americanhumanist.org/humanism/humanist-manifesto-ii/) — Paul Kurtz, Edwin H. Wilson · American Humanist Association · 1973  
  World community and transnational federal government, keeping national pride; no single state ideology; guaranteed annual income; reducing wealth disproportions; single ecosystem and population growth.  
  <sub>`aha/1973_humanist-manifesto-ii` · primary · web · verified 2026-10-10 by claude · cited in [lineage](research/lineage.md)</sub>
- [**Humanist Manifesto III: Humanism and Its Aspirations**](https://americanhumanist.org/humanism/humanist-manifesto-iii/) — American Humanist Association · 2003  
  Values subject to change as knowledge advances; 'global community'; just distribution of nature's resources; planetary duty to protect nature; 22 Nobel laureate signers.  
  <sub>`aha/2003_humanist-manifesto-iii` · primary · web · verified 2026-10-10 by claude · cited in [lineage](research/lineage.md)</sub>
- [**Letter dated 7 May 2010 from the Permanent Representative of the Plurinational State of Bolivia (A/64/777), annex II: Draft universal declaration of the rights of Mother Earth**](https://documents.un.org/doc/undoc/gen/n10/356/85/pdf/n1035685.pdf) — United Nations General Assembly · 2010-05-07  
  Cochabamba conference of 20-22 April 2010; the declaration's text (rights of all beings, freedom from cruelty, duties on States, 'the capitalist system'); 24 April date in the UN translation; call for General Assembly adoption.  
  <sub>`bolivia/2010_rights-of-mother-earth` · primary · report · verified 2026-10-10 by claude · cited in [lineage](research/lineage.md)</sub>
- [**Reference re Secession of Quebec, \[1998\] 2 SCR 217**](https://decisions.scc-csc.ca/scc-csc/scc-csc/en/item/1643/index.do) — Supreme Court of Canada · 1998-08-20  
  No unilateral secession; a clear majority on a clear question triggers a duty to negotiate; four underlying principles; primacy of the written text (para. 53); 'two legitimate majorities'.  
  <sub>`canada-scc/1998_secession-reference` · primary · court · verified 2026-10-10 by claude · cited in [living-charter](research/living-charter.md)</sub>
- [**Reference re Same-Sex Marriage, 2004 SCC 79**](https://decisions.scc-csc.ca/scc-csc/scc-csc/en/item/2196/index.do) — Supreme Court of Canada · 2004-12-09  
  The living tree as a fundamental interpretive principle (para. 22); Edwards' 'within its natural limits' quoted (para. 26); natural-limits argument rejected for lack of an objective core (paras. 27-28).  
  <sub>`canada-scc/2004_same-sex-marriage-reference` · primary · court · verified 2026-10-10 by claude · cited in [living-charter](research/living-charter.md)</sub>
- [**Canadian Charter of Rights and Freedoms (Constitution Act, 1982, Part I), section 33**](https://laws-lois.justice.gc.ca/eng/const/page-12.html) — Department of Justice Canada (Justice Laws Website) · 1982  
  s.33: legislatures may override certain rights; each declaration lapses after five years unless re-enacted.  
  <sub>`canada/1982_charter-of-rights` · primary · law · verified 2026-10-10 by claude · cited in [living-charter](research/living-charter.md)</sub>
- [**The Earth Charter**](https://earthcharter.org/read-the-earth-charter/) — Earth Charter Commission (published by Earth Charter International) · 2000-06-29  
  Preamble language; four pillars and 16 principles; precautionary burden of proof (principle 6); a common standard for all institutions; nations kept; population (principle 7).  
  <sub>`earth-charter/2000_earth-charter` · primary · web · verified 2026-10-10 by claude · cited in [lineage](research/lineage.md)</sub>
- [**The Amsterdam Declaration 2022**](https://humanists.international/what-is-humanism/the-amsterdam-declaration/) — Humanists International · 2022  
  Reason and compassion; duty of care to future generations and sentient beings; rule of law; 'no one is infallible'; refusal to impose its view; adoption at the Glasgow General Assembly.  
  <sub>`humanists-international/2022_amsterdam-declaration` · primary · web · verified 2026-10-10 by claude · cited in [lineage](research/lineage.md)</sub>
- [**Satanists Sue Netflix, Warner Bros. For $150M, Saying 'Sabrina' Copied Their Statue**](https://www.wjct.org/uncategorized/2018/11/satanists-sue-netflix-warner-bros-for-150m-saying-sabrina-copied-their-statue/) — Colin Dwyer · NPR (republished by WJCT Public Media) · 2018-11  
  TST sued Netflix and Warner Bros. in 2018 over its Baphomet statue design; Lucien Greaves described as co-founder.  
  <sub>`npr/2018_satanists-sue-netflix` · secondary · news · verified 2026-10-10 by claude · cited in [lineage](research/lineage.md)</sub>
- [**Unconstitutional Constitutional Amendments: A Study of the Nature and Limits of Constitutional Amendment Powers**](https://researchonline.lse.ac.uk/id/eprint/132337/1/Roznai_Unconstitutional-constitutional-amendments.pdf) — Yaniv Roznai · London School of Economics and Political Science (PhD thesis) · 2014  
  Counts of unamendable provisions (735 constitutions; 17%, 27%, 53%; 28% overall); Kesavananda 7-6 and case-by-case doctrine; Italy 1146/1988; no German amendment voided under Art. 79(3) as of 2014; Brazil ADI 939; dead-hand and revolution objections.  
  <sub>`roznai/2014_unconstitutional-amendments-thesis` · secondary · report · verified 2026-10-10 by claude · cited in [living-charter](research/living-charter.md)</sub>
- [**Cooperation and Commune Longevity: A Test of the Costly Signaling Theory of Religion**](https://richardsosis.com/wp-content/uploads/2023/01/2003-sosis-cooperation-and-commune-longevity-a-test.pdf) — Richard Sosis, Eric R. Bressler · Cross-Cultural Research · 2003-05  
  83 communes (30 religious, 53 secular); median life 25 vs 5 years; costly requirements predict longevity only in religious communes; causes of dissolution; Hutterites the sole survivor.

  s.39 interpretation clause ('spirit, purport and objects'); s.74 tiered, self-entrenched amendment rules (75% of the National Assembly and six provinces for s.1 and s.74(1)).

  Article 15: everyone has the right to a nationality; no one shall be arbitrarily deprived of it.; Art. 13: movement within each state and the right to leave any country; Art. 20: peaceful assembly and association.; Articles mapped to the floor (1, 2, 7, 19-23, 25-27); nationality (15); religion (18); international order (28); abuse of rights (30); proclamation by resolution 217 A.

## Part III — The Floor and the Ceiling

- [**The hole in the doughnut: Formalizing and testing a key model of degrowth**](https://onlinelibrary.wiley.com/doi/10.1111/coep.70031) — Ashruta Acharya, Vincent Geloso, Aleksander Psurek · Contemporary Economic Policy · 2026-04-02  
  Using economic freedom as a proxy for capitalism, little support for the doughnut model 'as here formalized'; relationship often reversed.  
  <sub>`acharya/2026_hole-in-the-doughnut` · primary · article · partial 2026-10-10 by claude: Abstract read via Crossref and the Wiley landing page (vol. 44(3):549-575); full text not read. · cited in [floor-and-ceiling](research/floor-and-ceiling.md)</sub>
- [**Are there holes in 'doughnut economics'? Kate Raworth takes on a major critic**](https://thealternative.org.uk/dailyalternative/2018/6/28/raworth-doughnuts-critics) — Pat Kane · The Alternative UK · 2018-06  
  Milanovic's four critiques as summarised by Raworth, including that global GDP 'must rise significantly to end poverty'; Raworth's reply that growth 'is very much needed' in low- and middle-income countries, home to 80% of people.  
  <sub>`alternative-uk/2018_raworth-reply-to-milanovic` · secondary · web · verified 2026-10-10 by claude · cited in [floor-and-ceiling](research/floor-and-ceiling.md)</sub>
- [**The Boundaries of the Planetary Boundary Framework: A Critical Appraisal of Approaches to Define a 'Safe Operating Space' for Humanity**](https://www.annualreviews.org/doi/pdf/10.1146/annurev-environ-012320-080337) — Frank Biermann, Rakhyun E. Kim · Annual Review of Environment and Resources · 2020-10  
  2009 group of 29 mostly natural scientists, no systematic civil-society or government input; shift of power to experts ('global expertocracy'); Earth Commission not UN-legitimated; 'decided by professors, not by the people'; weak Global South support; quotes Pickering and Persson.

  Cochabamba conference of 20-22 April 2010; the declaration's text (rights of all beings, freedom from cruelty, duties on States, 'the capitalist system'); 24 April date in the UN translation; call for General Assembly adoption.

  Bitcoin uses about 138 TWh of electricity a year, about 0.5% of global use, with 39.8 MtCO2e of emissions; a linked study puts Ethereum at 7.87 GWh a year.

  Annual doughnut 2000-2022; GDP more than doubled; deprivation progress must accelerate fivefold; richest 20% of countries over 40% of overshoot, poorest 40% over 60% of shortfall; ecological ring not comparably divisible by country; per capita shares for four boundaries; no traceable social-ecological link.

  Article 10: enforcement applies to individuals, including officials and corporate agents; police armed only to apprehend; searches and arrests on warrants from World Attorneys; attorneys answer to Parliament; non-military means. Article 9: judges elected for renewable ten-year terms. Article 11: Ombudsmus.; Adoption at Innsbruck (June 1977) and Troia amendments (May 1991); three-house parliament; Presidium and Cabinet with no veto; World Ombudsmus; warrant-based police armed only for apprehension; stage thresholds and disarmament by stage; paired ratification; non-aggression pledge; 25% turnout rule; exit zone after 90% of territory; 1995 stall review.; Art. VI: Presidium of 5 and Executive Cabinet of 20-30, all from parliament; one Presidium member per continental division; elected by parliament from nominees; 5-year terms, at most two in a row; removable for cause; no veto, no dissolution of parliament, no defiance of courts. Art. XI: council of five World Ombudsmen, elected by parliament, 10-year terms, may start court cases.; Arrest-only police; staged joining (WMD first, militaries last); also shows the text's own exit right.; World government may 'establish, issue and regulate world currency, credit and exchange' (Art. IV.17); a Planetary Banking System makes 'the transition to a common global currency' and funds government with interest-free credit (Art. VIII, Sec. G-1(e)-(f)).

- [**A Constitution for the Federation of Earth**](https://www.wcpa.global/en/constitution-for-the-federation-of-earth) — World Constitution and Parliament Association (text hosted by the Earth Constitution Institute) · 1977  
  Article 10: enforcement applies to individuals, including officials and corporate agents; police armed only to apprehend; searches and arrests on warrants from World Attorneys; attorneys answer to Parliament; non-military means. Article 9: judges elected for renewable ten-year terms. Article 11: Ombudsmus.; Adoption at Innsbruck (June 1977) and Troia amendments (May 1991); three-house parliament; Presidium and Cabinet with no veto; World Ombudsmus; warrant-based police armed only for apprehension; stage thresholds and disarmament by stage; paired ratification; non-aggression pledge; 25% turnout rule; exit zone after 90% of territory; 1995 stall review.; Art. VI: Presidium of 5 and Executive Cabinet of 20-30, all from parliament; one Presidium member per continental division; elected by parliament from nominees; 5-year terms, at most two in a row; removable for cause; no veto, no dissolution of parliament, no defiance of courts. Art. XI: council of five World Ombudsmen, elected by parliament, 10-year terms, may start court cases.; Arrest-only police; staged joining (WMD first, militaries last); also shows the text's own exit right.; World government may 'establish, issue and regulate world currency, credit and exchange' (Art. IV.17); a Planetary Banking System makes 'the transition to a common global currency' and funds government with interest-free credit (Art. VIII, Sec. G-1(e)-(f)).

- [**A Constitution for the Federation of Earth**](https://www.wcpa.global/en/constitution-for-the-federation-of-earth) — World Constitution and Parliament Association (text hosted by the Earth Constitution Institute) · 1977  
  Article 10: enforcement applies to individuals, including officials and corporate agents; police armed only to apprehend; searches and arrests on warrants from World Attorneys; attorneys answer to Parliament; non-military means. Article 9: judges elected for renewable ten-year terms. Article 11: Ombudsmus.; Adoption at Innsbruck (June 1977) and Troia amendments (May 1991); three-house parliament; Presidium and Cabinet with no veto; World Ombudsmus; warrant-based police armed only for apprehension; stage thresholds and disarmament by stage; paired ratification; non-aggression pledge; 25% turnout rule; exit zone after 90% of territory; 1995 stall review.; Art. VI: Presidium of 5 and Executive Cabinet of 20-30, all from parliament; one Presidium member per continental division; elected by parliament from nominees; 5-year terms, at most two in a row; removable for cause; no veto, no dissolution of parliament, no defiance of courts. Art. XI: council of five World Ombudsmen, elected by parliament, 10-year terms, may start court cases.; Arrest-only police; staged joining (WMD first, militaries last); also shows the text's own exit right.; World government may 'establish, issue and regulate world currency, credit and exchange' (Art. IV.17); a Planetary Banking System makes 'the transition to a common global currency' and funds government with interest-free credit (Art. VIII, Sec. G-1(e)-(f)).  
  <sub>`wcpa/1977_earth-constitution` · primary · report · verified 2026-10-10 by claude · cited in [accountability](research/accountability.md), [commonwealth](research/commonwealth.md), [deliberation](research/deliberation.md), [drafting-session-checks](research/drafting-session-checks.md), [money](research/money.md)</sub>

## Part XI — A Living Charter

- [**Humanist Manifesto III: Humanism and Its Aspirations**](https://americanhumanist.org/humanism/humanist-manifesto-iii/) — American Humanist Association · 2003  
  Values subject to change as knowledge advances; 'global community'; just distribution of nature's resources; planetary duty to protect nature; 22 Nobel laureate signers.  
  <sub>`aha/2003_humanist-manifesto-iii` · primary · web · verified 2026-10-10 by claude · cited in [lineage](research/lineage.md)</sub>
- [**The Boundaries of the Planetary Boundary Framework: A Critical Appraisal of Approaches to Define a 'Safe Operating Space' for Humanity**](https://www.annualreviews.org/doi/pdf/10.1146/annurev-environ-012320-080337) — Frank Biermann, Rakhyun E. Kim · Annual Review of Environment and Resources · 2020-10  
  2009 group of 29 mostly natural scientists, no systematic civil-society or government input; shift of power to experts ('global expertocracy'); Earth Commission not UN-legitimated; 'decided by professors, not by the people'; weak Global South support; quotes Pickering and Persson.  
  <sub>`biermann/2020_boundaries-of-pb-framework` · secondary · article · verified 2026-10-10 by claude · cited in [floor-and-ceiling](research/floor-and-ceiling.md)</sub>
- [**Brazil's Constitution of 1988 with Amendments through 2017**](https://www.constituteproject.org/constitution/Brazil_2017.pdf?lang=en) — Constitute Project · 1988  
  Art. 60 §2 (three-fifths in two rounds) and §4 (subjects that may not be abolished, including individual rights).  
  <sub>`brazil/1988_constitution` · primary · law · verified 2026-10-10 by claude · cited in [living-charter](research/living-charter.md)</sub>
- [**Constituição da República Federativa do Brasil de 1988 (texto compilado)**](https://www.planalto.gov.br/ccivil_03/constituicao/constituicao.htm) — Presidência da República (Planalto) · 2026  
  The official compiled text cites Constitutional Amendment No. 139 of 2026, showing frequent amendment despite Art. 60 §4.

  Texts contrary to superior law, including human rights, may not be put to a vote; advisable to have no turnout or approval quorum, since a turnout quorum rewards abstention; approval quorum acceptable for fundamental constitutional matters.; Advises against turnout and approval quorums; accepts an approval quorum or qualified majority for matters of fundamental constitutional significance (including secession where allowed), and multiple majorities for revisions in federal states.

- [**A Constitution for the Federation of Earth**](https://www.wcpa.global/en/constitution-for-the-federation-of-earth) — World Constitution and Parliament Association (text hosted by the Earth Constitution Institute) · 1977  
  Article 10: enforcement applies to individuals, including officials and corporate agents; police armed only to apprehend; searches and arrests on warrants from World Attorneys; attorneys answer to Parliament; non-military means. Article 9: judges elected for renewable ten-year terms. Article 11: Ombudsmus.; Adoption at Innsbruck (June 1977) and Troia amendments (May 1991); three-house parliament; Presidium and Cabinet with no veto; World Ombudsmus; warrant-based police armed only for apprehension; stage thresholds and disarmament by stage; paired ratification; non-aggression pledge; 25% turnout rule; exit zone after 90% of territory; 1995 stall review.; Art. VI: Presidium of 5 and Executive Cabinet of 20-30, all from parliament; one Presidium member per continental division; elected by parliament from nominees; 5-year terms, at most two in a row; removable for cause; no veto, no dissolution of parliament, no defiance of courts. Art. XI: council of five World Ombudsmen, elected by parliament, 10-year terms, may start court cases.; Arrest-only police; staged joining (WMD first, militaries last); also shows the text's own exit right.; World government may 'establish, issue and regulate world currency, credit and exchange' (Art. IV.17); a Planetary Banking System makes 'the transition to a common global currency' and funds government with interest-free credit (Art. VIII, Sec. G-1(e)-(f)).  
  <sub>`wcpa/1977_earth-constitution` · primary · report · verified 2026-10-10 by claude · cited in [accountability](research/accountability.md), [commonwealth](research/commonwealth.md), [deliberation](research/deliberation.md), [drafting-session-checks](research/drafting-session-checks.md), [money](research/money.md)</sub>

- [**A Constitution for the Federation of Earth**](https://www.wcpa.global/en/constitution-for-the-federation-of-earth) — World Constitution and Parliament Association (text hosted by the Earth Constitution Institute) · 1977  
  Article 10: enforcement applies to individuals, including officials and corporate agents; police armed only to apprehend; searches and arrests on warrants from World Attorneys; attorneys answer to Parliament; non-military means. Article 9: judges elected for renewable ten-year terms. Article 11: Ombudsmus.; Adoption at Innsbruck (June 1977) and Troia amendments (May 1991); three-house parliament; Presidium and Cabinet with no veto; World Ombudsmus; warrant-based police armed only for apprehension; stage thresholds and disarmament by stage; paired ratification; non-aggression pledge; 25% turnout rule; exit zone after 90% of territory; 1995 stall review.; Art. VI: Presidium of 5 and Executive Cabinet of 20-30, all from parliament; one Presidium member per continental division; elected by parliament from nominees; 5-year terms, at most two in a row; removable for cause; no veto, no dissolution of parliament, no defiance of courts. Art. XI: council of five World Ombudsmen, elected by parliament, 10-year terms, may start court cases.; Arrest-only police; staged joining (WMD first, militaries last); also shows the text's own exit right.; World government may 'establish, issue and regulate world currency, credit and exchange' (Art. IV.17); a Planetary Banking System makes 'the transition to a common global currency' and funds government with interest-free credit (Art. VIII, Sec. G-1(e)-(f)).
